Key plan Key plan Fordham University New Residence Halls, New York City (Sasaki) Traffic calming measures and angled parking (various)

Calm streets

Slow residential streets designed with slow traffic measures and speed restriction to naturally slow down vehicle speeds to enhance pedestrian safety, reduce noise, and encourage a balance between modes of transport

Controls

— Adopt lower speed limits of 40 km/h — Include, retain, or improve verge — Apply mid-block pinch points in the form of curb extensions — Use flat-top road pavements to slow traffic and provide pedestrian crossing points — Provide angled parking on one side of the street and parallel parking on the other to maximise on-road parking capacity

Green pedestrian links

Narrow pedestrian-priority streets within residential areas that provides connectivity to adjacent residential streets while acting as green refuges allowing for passive recreation

Controls

— Minimum width of 9m (shared between developments) — Allow visibility along the length and open to the sky — Implement continuous footpath treatment — Incoporate linear public open space along both sides of the link with tree planting and water- sensitive urban design — Provide street furniture and incoporate passive recreation elements such as small play features

Primary active frontages

### Appendix B

### Community infrastructure delivery strategy

Community infrastructure delivery strategy

The masterplan leverages local planning provisions applicable to key development sites to facilitate the provision of community infrastructure as part of privately initiated developments. This approach is complemented by infrastructure delivery led by Council on Council-owned or dedicated land, ensuring a coordinated and comprehensive outcome across the precinct. Within the proposed civic precinct, the following community facilities are to be delivered:

— A new Mosman Library, incorporating a youth centre, senior centre and flexible community spaces — Public parking provisions to support increased activity and accessibility — An expanded and enhanced Village Green (approximately 2,000 sqm), featuring an upgraded playground and an outdoor performance stage

To support delivery outcomes, local planning provisions will include incentive Floor Space Ratio (FSR) mechanisms for key sites. These incentives are intended to facilitate the provision of:

— Three indoor sports courts — Two additional community facilities, or alternatively, additional affordable housing — A 2,000 sqm publicly accessible open space at 6 Hale Road (The Manors of Mosman site) — A 1,500 sqm publicly accessible open space at the Bridgepoint Shopping Centre — Publicly accessible through site links breaking down long blocks increasing pedestrian accessibility and permeability

This integrated framework aligns private development potential with public benefit, ensuring the timely and equitable delivery of essential community infrastructure. Sacred Hear Catholic Primary School

The Mosman LGA has an existing tree canopy cover of 30 to 35%. A tree canopy cover of 40% is recommended to ensure that existing landscape and trees are retained and protected, while new trees are introduced to increase canopy cover by 3%.

The following principles are recommended for new development in relation to tree canopy cover:

— Maximise tree canopy and green cover on streets, in open spaces and along the public domain. — Create a contiguous tree canopy to mitigate climate change and improve the health and wellbeing of people and ecosystems. — Prioritise native local species of vegetation . — Collaborate with local aboriginal custodians to highlight first nations connection to Mosman through vegetation. — Integrate Water Sensitive Urban Design measures in streets and spaces to harness rainwater to promote vegetation growth.

Tree canopy targets can be achieved by:

6. Protecting and strengthening existing trees and vegetation in Mosman. 7. Implementing recommended controls for deep soil and landscaping. 8. Requiring new development within the Mosman LGA to deliver 40% of the site area as landscaped area. Landscaped area means a part of a site used for growing plants, grasses and trees, but does not include any building, structure or hard-paved area. 9. For any on-street car parking, interspersed tree pits should be placed as follows: — one tree pit for every three end-on-end car spaces; — one tree pit for every five parallel car spaces.

### Appendix C

### Assumptions

Existing planning capacity under the current LEP

Existing theoretical planning capacity has been calculated having regard to the Stage 1 Low to Mid Rise (LMR) Housing reforms, as applied to Mosman LGA.

The capacity estimate is based on the following zone-specific yield assumptions:

R2:

— Lots that meet the minimum lot size required for dual occupancies are assumed capable of delivering two ( 2 ) dwellings per lot. — Lots that do not meet the minimum lot size for dual occupancies are assumed to deliver one ( 1 )  dwelling per lot.

C4 Environmental Living:

— Dwelling homes are the only permissible typology; accordingly, a yield of one ( 1 ) dwelling per lot is applied.

R3 and E3:

— Capacity is derived from the permissible Floor Space Ratio (FSR), noting that residential flat buildings are permissible within these zones. Yield is therefore calculated on an FSR-based approach.

E1:

— Where shop-top housing is permissible, residential capacity is calculated by applying a non-residential FSR allowance of 0.6:1, with the balance of permissible floor space assumed to be available for residential use (shop-top housing)

Planning capacity under the LMR

LMR “application land” was independently delineated in accordance with the published LMR eligibility criteria. This independent mapping was cross-checked against the Department of Planning, Housing and Infrastructure (DPHI) LMR application land mapping and no material inconsistencies were identified.

The Affordable Housing Bonus was excluded from the housing capacity estimate. Accordingly, all dwelling yields reported represent capacity under the base LMR controls only.

LMR does not apply to heritage listed items. Heritage items have been excluded from the calculation.

LMR planning capacity has been expressed as a range for R2 Low Density Residential land, using the maximum Floor Space Ratio (FSR) applicable to low-rise housing and mid-rise housing under the LMR controls. This approach is informed by a review of recent development applications (DAs) in Mosman which indicates that low-rise typologies are currently the predominant and most viable development outcome within the R2 zone.

All other applicable LMR development standards were applied to eligible R2 and R3 land within the mapped 400 m and 800 m catchments .

Average unit size

Under existing LEP controls, the average unit size is derived from Mosman’s current housing stock. This ensures the calculation reflects established development patterns and the typical unit configurations found in existing strata buildings. An analysis of local strata schemes was used to determine representative unit sizes for estimating dwelling yields in the E1, E3 and R3 zones.

Adopted average unit size (Existing LEP controls): 105 m² per dwelling

Under the LMR controls, the average unit size adopted for yield estimation is based on lodged development applications within Mosman. Lodged proposals provide the most direct evidence of what proponents consider feasible within current market conditions and development standards, and therefore offer a robust basis for modelling likely dwelling configurations under the LMR controls. The reviewed lodged applications were used to derive a representative average unit size for converting permissible floor space into dwelling yields under the LMR scenario and this equates to an average unit size (LMR controls): 163 m² per dwelling.

The average unit size adopted to establish 4,700 dwellings is an average between the two and equates to 134 m²average dwelling size.

Sieving process

Strata over 10 lots:  Lots within strata schemes comprising 10 or more lots were excluded on the basis that fragmented ownership typically requires extensive negotiation, time, and financial alignment to secure collective agreement, and this transaction burden is generally disproportionate to the marginal uplift offered under the LMR controls. Below 10, with LMR FSR of 2.2:1 is assumed feasible (this assumption has been made by SJB, it is not based on economics or independent feasibility study).

Community infrastructure: Sites held in public ownership, community title, and/or with an infrastructure/community function (including land zoned SP2 or equivalent special-purpose categories) were excluded due to their high community value, long-term operational role, and limited redevelopment flexibility

Recent developments: Lots subject to recent and significant development investment were excluded as they are unlikely to redevelop within the relevant assessment horizon given remaining building life and sunk costs, and because the uplift available under the LMR controls is typically insufficient to trigger redevelopment.

Flood-affected land: A flood study was carried out by Council in 2025. The report has been used to asses flood affected zone.

Flood-affected lots have been assessed using Flood Planning Constraint Categories (FPCC).

— FPCC1-2 High risk flood areas: Any lot intersecting these zones is excluded — FPCC3 Moderate flood risk: Only lots significant affected are excluded, based on advice from Council engineers. Lots with minor overlaps with FPCC3 zones are retained to avoid the exclusion of otherwise viable sites.

August 2026

Attachment 1.1.1 Mosman Council

### Commercial workspace calculation

A study of the commercial built form across Mosman has been undertaken estimate the number of commercial jobs that may be affected by the masterplan. The analysis focuses on observed commercial development throughout the LGA to provide an indicative assessment of employment capacity.

The methodology involves analysing built‑form data, including floor plate area and number of storeys, to calculate the gross building area (GBA) for each identified commercial building. A range of efficiency assumptions is then applied to derive gross floor area (GFA), which forms the basis for estimating employment capacity.

Employment estimates are generated by applying workspace ratios to the calculated GFA. This approach is informed by the Common Planning Assumptions (CPA) 2023 Workspace Ratios 2023 published by the NSW Department of Planning. These ratios are used to estimate the number of full‑time equivalent jobs that may be supported by commercial development, based on the business size and land use typology.

The analysis is based on observed commercial built form within predominantly non-residential areas. Employment generated by other uses have not been fully captured. Notable exclusions include Taronga Zoo, HMAS Penguin Naval Base, and religious institutions, which are unaffected by the masterplan.

### Executive Summary

KEY OUTCOMES FOR THE SITE

#### KEY OUTCOMES

#### ~4,000m² Library & Community Hub

A contemporary civic destination for learning, gathering and community life.

#### ~2,000m² Village Green

Expanded public open space supporting events, recreation and community activity.

#### New Community, Retail +

#### Commercial Uses

A diverse mix of uses supporting activation and precinct vitality.

#### Consolidated Public Parking

Parking consolidated underground to support future growth and place activation.

#### One Connected Civic Heart

Connecting Mosman’s key civic, cultural and recreational destinations

The Mosman Civic Precinct presents a once-in-a-generation opportunity to strengthen Mosman’s civic heart through a connected mix of community, commercial and public spaces.

Building on the precinct’s established civic role, the Mosman Civic Precinct Place Plan envisions a new Library & Community Hub, an expanded Village Green, enhanced public spaces and improved connections between Mosman Square, Mosman Art Gallery and Allan Border Oval.

As Mosman’s population continues to grow and diversify, the precinct will provide new opportunities for community gathering, learning, recreation and civic life. A connected landscape network, activated public spaces, and a mix of community, cultural, retail and commercial uses will support a vibrant and welcoming destination that serves current and future generations.

Together, these initiatives will create a connected civic centre that supports local activity while advancing the long-term vision established by the Mosman Masterplan.

### Purpose of the Report

A visual representation of the preferred precinct structure, illustrating the intended arrangement of buildings, open spaces, landscape and movement.

A framework of urban design principles and planning controls that guide future built form, landscape, movement and public domain outcomes.

An indicative development outcome demonstrating how the Place Plan framework can be realised through a coordinated ground-floor, landscape and public domain response.

### DESIGN GUIDELINES

### REFERENCE SCHEME

### ILLUSTRATED PLACE PLAN

This Place Plan has been prepared to guide the future evolution of the Mosman Civic Precinct. It establishes a coordinated urban design framework for built form, landscape, movement, and public domain, providing clear direction for future planning, design and implementation while supporting a high-quality civic outcome.

Responding to the broader transformation anticipated through the Mosman Masterplan, the Place Plan strengthens Mosman’s civic, cultural and community heart while respecting the precinct’s valued character.

Design Guidelines

The Design Guidelines establish the key urban design principles and built form framework that will guide future development within the precinct. They provide direction on building height and massing, movement, landscape, public domain, active frontages and civic interfaces to ensure future development contributes to a cohesive, high-quality and people-focused environment.

Illustrated Place Plan

The Illustrated Place Plan translates the Design Guidelines into a spatial vision for the precinct. It illustrates the intended arrangement of buildings, streets, open spaces and landscape, communicating the desired future character and demonstrating how the design principles shape a connected and vibrant civic destination.

Reference Scheme

The Reference Scheme demonstrates one potential development outcome informed by the Place Plan framework. It provides greater clarity around the proposed ground-floor arrangement, landscape and public domain, illustrating how the precinct’s different zones and intended uses can work together to realise the vision and objectives of the Place Plan.

Staging plan overlaid on the future Mosman Masterplan context.

The Civic Precinct Place Plan is intended to be delivered in stages, providing a clear framework for the coordinated renewal of the precinct over time.

Stage 1 (0-5 years)

Establishes the civic centre through delivery of key community facilities and public realm improvements:

— Deliver the expanded Library & Community Hub — Consolidate existing at-grade parking into basement parking, reproviding existing supply, accommodating future demand and allowing flexibility for potential future commercial uses — Deliver upgrades to the existing Mosman Art Gallery and its surrounding public realm — Establish a new shared road from Mosman Square to the Mosman Art Gallery, extending the Art Gallery’s public domain and shared zone along Art Gallery Way. — Expand and enhance the Village Green as the precinct’s primary civic open space — Pedestrianise The Crescent to prioritise the public realm and improve pedestrian connectivity to Allan Border Oval

Stage 2 (5-15 years)

Strengthens the Military Road interface through new commercial development and supporting infrastructure: — Deliver a new mixed-use commercial building with a public arcade connecting Military Road to the new Library & Community Hub — Expand basement parking to support future development — *This plan assumes the acquisition or co-development of an adjoining site because this maximises the public benefit outcome and preserves adjacent heritage values. Should this not proceed, an amended Stage 2 is feasible and would be progressed working solely in Council’s own land holdings.

Stages 3 & 4 (15-30 years)

Completes the long-term vision for the Civic Precinct through additional mixed-use redevelopment and future growth:

— Redevelop the Council Chambers site as a mixed-use destination — Enable potential future development above the retained heritage terraces fronting Military Road (subject to feasibility and heritage considerations)

Mosman Civic Precinct Place Plan 210 SJB

### Urban Role of the Civic Precinct

The Civic Precinct is one of Mosman Village Centre’s primary public anchors. Located at the northern gateway to the Military Road Conservation Area, it marks the transition into the village’s heritage main street and plays a defining role in Mosman’s civic and cultural identity.

As one of the largest areas of Council-owned land within the village centre, the precinct brings together key existing public assets including Council Chambers, the Library, the Council car park, the Village Green and Mosman Square. Together, these facilities support everyday community services, civic functions, public events and informal gathering, making the precinct the heart of civic life in Mosman.

The precinct is also strategically positioned between Allan Border Oval, Military Road, Bridgepoint Shopping Centre, Mosman Art Gallery and the public swim centre, placing it at the centre of a broader network of civic, cultural, recreational and commercial destinations within walking distance.

This concentration of publicly owned land presents a rare opportunity to coordinate future civic investment, strengthen connections between these destinations and deliver a more integrated, vibrant and accessible civic precinct for the community.

Existing civic, cultural and community context within the wider Mosman Village Centre.

### Context

The Mosman Civic Precinct forms an important civic and community hub within Mosman Town Centre, bringing together a mix of civic, cultural, community and open space uses.

Key characteristics of the precinct include:

— Central location within the Mosman Town Centre — Mix of civic, cultural, community and recreational uses — Strong landscape setting anchored by the Village Green and Allan  Border Oval — Existing civic buildings set within a fine-grain surrounding context — Network of public spaces, streets and pedestrian connections — Existing level changes impacting accessibility and connectivity — Underutilised Council landholdings with opportunity for renewal

Together, these conditions position the precinct as a significant opportunity to strengthen Mosman’s civic identity, improve connectivity and enhance the relationship between community uses and public open space.

### Site Context Photos

The Civic Precinct is characterised by mature tree canopy, heritage buildings and well-established civic and community facilities. While the precinct contains high-quality public spaces such as Mosman Square and the Village Green, other areas are fragmented by surface car parking, poor pedestrian connections and underutilised land. This section of Military Road is characterised by a fine-grain heritage streetscape that transitions into the civic and commercial character of Mosman Square. Active ground-floor retail with upper-level commercial and residential uses creates a vibrant pedestrian environment, while the adjoining public square and signalised intersection establishes a key arrival point to the Civic Precinct.

This section of Military Road is defined by a strong mixed-use edge with active ground-floor retail and residential uses above. Mature street trees, generous footpaths and on-street parking supports a comfortable pedestrian environment, while the emerging five-storey built form establishes a contemporary urban frontage that reinforces the precinct’s role as Mosman’s civic and commercial centre.

The site is constrained by a range of physical, planning and operational factors that influence the location, scale and delivery of future development.

KEY

Site boundary

Council-owned land

Heritage item

Re-provide existing parking Existing Council Chambers building +RL86.6 Topography (RLs)

Site constraints within the existing context.

Swim Centre

Village Green

HERITAGE

Heritage-listed buildings, the Heritage Conservation Area and sensitive interfaces with adjoining heritage items constrain redevelopment and require a considered built form response.

TOPOGRAPHY

The site’s sloping topography, falling from Military Road to The Crescent, requires level changes to be addressed when considering building siting, accessibility, public domain and basement design.

SOLAR ACCESS

The proposed solar access controls, which require no additional overshadowing of Allan Border Oval, constrain building height and massing across the site.

ACCESS, SERVICING & EASEMENTS

The existing road network, property access, servicing arrangements and easements, including access to the heritage terraces fronting Military Road and adjoining residential properties accessed from Myahgah Road, must be maintained throughout redevelopment.

EXISTING CIVIC BUILDINGS

Existing civic buildings, including the Council Chambers, are expected to remain in the short term, influencing redevelopment staging and site planning.

PUBLIC PARKING

The existing public parking provision must be re-provided to support the ongoing operation of the civic precinct.

SITE OWNERSHIP

The existing pattern of land ownership, with Council owning only part of the precinct, influences redevelopment extent, staging and delivery. The development configuration in this report assumes a potential future acquisition or co-development of adjoining land. If acquisition or co-development does not proceed, an alternative scheme remains feasible within Council-owned sites.

Library & Community Hub

Redevelop and expand the library to create a stronger civic destination, supporting a broader range of community, cultural and civic uses.

VILLAGE GREEN

Expand and enhance the Village Green as the heart of the precinct, creating a larger, more flexible civic open space with upgraded play opportunities and a new performance area.

CONNECTIVITY & PERMEABILITY

Strengthen pedestrian connectivity by reinforcing a north–south spine through Mosman Square, improving permeability and strengthening connections between new development and surrounding community destinations, including the Swim Centre, Art Gallery and Allan Border Oval.

PUBLIC REALM

Integrate Mosman Square with future development while improving the public realm along The Crescent and Myahgah Road, including opportunities for future pedestrianisation of The Crescent.

HERITAGE

Celebrate the heritage terraces along Military Road by retaining their frontages. Prioritise sensitive redevelopment that responds to the precinct’s heritage significance and surrounding heritage interfaces.

BUILT FORM

Locate higher-density development along key civic edges to reinforce the precinct structure, respond to the future Mosman Masterplan and provide an appropriate transition to surrounding heritage and residential interfaces.

UNDERGROUND PARKING

Consolidate parking below ground to prioritise public realm, improve pedestrian amenity and support future redevelopment.

KEY VIEWS

Protect and enhance key views through the precinct, maintaining visual connections to Allan Border Oval, Mosman Square and surrounding civic destinations.

### Program Opportunities

The Civic Precinct has the potential to accommodate a diverse mix of uses and activities that support its role as a civic, community and mixed-use destination. The following uses illustrate the broad range of activities that may support the long- term evolution of the precinct. They are intended to inform future testing rather than represent a proposed development program.

These potential uses can be understood along a spectrum ranging from public, civic and community-focused activities through to more private or commercially-oriented uses.

While all uses may be appropriate in principle, each carries different operational, spatial and delivery considerations. These include variations in:

— ownership and management models — ground-floor activation needs — servicing requirements — parking demand — access arrangements — security — interface conditions with the public realm

Given the scale and capacity of the precinct, it is not anticipated that all identified uses should be delivered simultaneously. Rather, the program opportunities illustrate the range of potential use combinations that could support the future evolution of the precinct. The preferred mix of uses should balance civic value, community benefit, commercial viability, operational practicality and Council’s long-term strategic objectives.

As the Civic Precinct Place Plan progresses, further work will refine these opportunities into a preferred mix of uses to inform a future project brief.

### Design Objectives

Protect solar access to Allan Border Oval, with built form shaped to minimise overshadowing and protect midwinter sun.

Provide a clear transition in height and bulk to surrounding residential and conservation areas, stepping down at sensitive edges.

Enhance and extend the Village Green as a key civic space for events, recreation and everyday use, including a new performance area, playground and flexible event lawn.

### MAINTAIN SOLAR ACCESS

### TRANSITION BUILDING HEIGHT

### EXTEND VILLAGE GREEN

Limit vehicle access within the precinct by consolidating parking underground and prioritising pedestrian movement throughout the public realm.

Reinforce Mosman’s village character through sensitive architecture, landscape character and carefully integrated heritage.

Provide new and improved pedestrian connections to strengthen permeability and connect into the broader network.

Deliver active frontages to support a safe, vibrant, and engaging civic domain.

Mosman Civic Precinct Place Plan 227 SJB

### Key Moves

Extend the landscape

Draw the surrounding parklands into the precinct by extending the landscape network through the site. The pedestrianisation of The Crescent strengthens connections between Mosman Square, the Village Green and Allan Border Oval, while the new and expanded Village Green offers a generous, flexible civic space for events, recreation and everyday community life.

Library & Community Hub at the Heart

A new Library & Community Hub is proposed in its existing location, reinforcing its role as the civic heart of the precinct. Positioned adjacent to the Village Green, the building anchors the primary civic space, strengthens connections between key community destinations and creates an active, welcoming and highly visible civic destination.

Flexible civic open space. Library integrated with landscape and community gathering spaces.

### Design Concept

August 2026

<!-- page 232 -->

Mosman Civic Precinct Place Plan 228 SJB

### Key Moves

Connected Civic Network

New and enhanced through-site links strengthen connections between the Library & Community Hub, Village Green, Mosman Square, Mosman Art Gallery, and surrounding community destinations. Together, these connections establish a legible civic network that improves permeability and integrates the precinct with the surrounding pedestrian network.

Defined Development Zones

Development is concentrated within defined building zones that frame the expanded Village Green, reinforce key civic connections and position the Library & Community Hub as the precinct’s primary civic anchor. Building envelopes respond to heritage interfaces, key view corridors and the solar clipping planes protecting Allan Border Oval.

Pedestrian-focused civic connection. Built form framing civic open space.

August 2026

Attachment 1.1.1 Mosman Council

![Figure on page 232](../images/0e82f8bec2302ff3.webp)

<!-- page 233 -->

Mosman Civic Precinct Place Plan 229 SJB

### Key Moves

Sensitive Height Transition

Height is concentrated along Military Road, responding to its role as a primary corridor and the emerging taller built form along this edge. Built form transitions down towards Allan Border Oval, balancing increased density with neighbourhood character while protecting amenity at sensitive interfaces.

Contextual Podium Response

Podium and street wall heights respond to the surrounding context, reinforcing the character and scale of adjacent heritage and civic buildings. Lower built form aligns with established datum lines and street wall conditions, maintaining a consistent human scale while reinforcing active civic edges.

Podium responding to neighbourhood character. Graduated building height transition.

### Civic Place Plan

### Design Guidelines - Landscape & Public Domain

Design Guidelines - Landscape & Public Domain Plan.

Design Guidelines:

— Expand and raise the existing Village Green to create a minimum 2,000m² of contiguous civic open space above a semi-basement car park. — Design Village Green levels to provide a gradual transition between Mosman Square, the Library & Community Hub, and The Crescent, integrating landscaped terraces, paths and steps that respond to changes in topography. — Provide a children’s playground along the northern edge of the Village Green to create a shaded play environment. — Establish a flexible central open lawn to support everyday informal recreation, community gatherings, markets and events. — Deliver a low-profile outdoor performance space integrated within the landscaped terraces of the Village Green to preserve views towards Allan Border Oval. — Integrate outdoor seating, dining spill-out areas and informal gathering spaces around the edges of the Village Green and The Crescent to support cafes, the Library & Community Hub and everyday social activity.

Design Guidelines:

— Extend the landscape character and public domain treatment of Mosman Square through The Crescent and Myahgah Road to establish a consistent civic identity. — Reinforce the civic identity of the precinct through a coordinated palette of paving, planting, lighting, furniture and street trees. — Retain significant existing trees wherever practicable and integrate them into the landscape and public domain design. Provide additional setbacks and built form articulation where required to facilitate tree retention. — Provide landscaped podium rooftops overlooking Allan Border Oval to reinforce the precinct’s landscape character. — Enhance the Mosman Art Gallery setting through landscape upgrades, extending the Gallery’s open space along Art Gallery Way and enabling temporary closure for events.

Design Guidelines:

— Pedestrianise The Crescent to create a car-free civic space that strengthens the interface between the Library & Community Hub and Allan Border Oval. — Establish the nominated section of Myahgah Road & Art Gallery Way as a shared movement zone, prioritising pedestrians while accommodating local access and servicing for adjoining residential properties. — Design primary pedestrian routes with generous widths, high-quality paving, lighting and clear wayfinding to accommodate anticipated pedestrian volumes. — Integrate future Art Gallery landscape upgrades with the new shared zone along Myahgah Road and Art Gallery Way to create a seamless public domain connection. — Provide a short- to medium-term civic forecourt outside the existing Council Chambers entrance as an interim civic space prior to future redevelopment of the site. — Provide accessible paths, ramps and landscaped terraces that respond to the site’s topography, ensuring equitable access throughout the precinct.

Design Guidelines:

— Reinforce Mosman Square as the precinct’s principal civic spine by strengthening pedestrian connections to Military Road, the Village Green, Allan Border Oval and the Mosman Art Gallery. — Provide a network of secondary pedestrian connections linking Mosman Square, the Library & Community Hub, the Village Green and surrounding civic destinations, to create safe and legible routes throughout the precinct. — Provide internal pedestrian arcades, including an east–west connection through the Council Chambers site and a north–south connection between Military Road, the Library & Community Hub and The Crescent. — Terminate the primary east-west pedestrian connection at a prominent Library & Community Hub entrance, reinforcing the library as a key civic destination. — Principal building entries should address key pedestrian routes and civic spaces, including Mosman Square, Military Road and the connection between the Library & Community Hub and Village Green. — Align north–south pedestrian connections to maintain key views from Military Road and Mosman Square towards the Allan Border Oval & its Pavilion, reinforcing wayfinding and civic legibility.

Design Guidelines:

— Pedestrianise The Crescent to create a car-free civic space between the Village Green and Allan Border Oval, prioritising pedestrian movement and public life. — Establish the nominated section of Myahgah Road & Art Gallery Way as a shared movement zone, prioritising pedestrians while accommodating local access and servicing for adjoining residential properties. — Provide accessible pedestrian connections throughout the precinct that respond to changes in topography through integrated paths, ramps and landscaped steps. — Provide generous civic forecourts and car-free pedestrian interfaces at key destinations, including the Library & Community Hub, Village Green and Mosman Square.

Design Guidelines:

— Consolidate public parking within a basement beneath future development to minimise vehicle infrastructure at ground level. The basement footprint should be designed to support future staged expansion, allowing integration with adjoining basement parking as the precinct evolves. — Locate the basement entry at street level from The Crescent, beneath the south-east corner of the new Library & Community Hub, to minimise excavation and provide efficient access for vehicles, servicing and loading. — Provide a turning circle adjacent to the basement vehicle entry to facilitate safe and efficient vehicle manoeuvring, servicing, loading and emergency access. — Provide flexibility for potential future commercial uses within the Stage 1 basement footprint, subject to future detailed design.

Design Guidelines:

— Retain the Library & Community Hub in its existing location, reinforcing its role as the precinct’s primary civic anchor while defining and addressing the Village Green. — Establish the Library & Community Hub as the precinct’s principal civic landmark through its siting, architectural expression, and its relationship to the Village Green and Allan Border Oval. — Orient principal building entrances towards key active frontages and civic spaces, including a primary entry addressing the car-free Crescent, to maximise visibility, accessibility and civic presence. — Design the outdoor performance space as a visually permeable civic structure that supports community events, performances and civic gatherings while activating the Village Green.

Design Guidelines:

— Support flexible community events, markets, performances and everyday recreation within the Village Green, reinforcing its role as the precinct’s primary civic gathering space. — Integrate a children’s playground within the Village Green to support families and provide opportunities for informal play. — Integrate public art opportunities within the landscaped edges surrounding the central open lawn to enrich the Village Green and celebrate Mosman’s civic and cultural identity.

Design Guidelines:

— Provide active frontages along the Village Green, Mosman Square, Military Road, internal arcades, and the Library & Community Hub. Active frontages should include retail, food and beverage, and other publicly accessible uses at ground level, with the final mix of uses informed through future design development. — Design active frontages with frequent entries, transparent glazing and primary building entrances addressing key public spaces and pedestrian routes — Introduce a short- to medium-term civic forecourt outside the existing Council Chambers entrance to support civic activity until future redevelopment of the site. — Incorporate integrated public art opportunities within building facades along the north–south civic connection between Military Road and the Library & Community Hub to strengthen the pedestrian experience and reinforce the precinct’s civic identity. — Locate community and civic uses at ground level around the Village Green and Mosman Square, complemented by commercial uses along Military Road, to create an active and complementary precinct.

#### TRANSITION BUILDING HEIGHT

#### MAINTAIN SOLAR ACCESS

#### RESPECT VILLAGE CHARACTER

Design Guidelines:

— Shape building heights in accordance with the Allan Border Oval Solar Clipping Plane to protect solar access and establish an appropriate height transition across the precinct. — Locate the Library & Community Hub as a maximum four-storey building to protect solar access to Allan Border Oval while reinforcing its civic role fronting the Village Green. — Concentrate taller built form ranging from 5 to 10 storeys along Military Road, stepping down towards neighbouring heritage properties and Allan Border Oval. — Provide upper level setbacks determined by the Solar Clipping Plane (SCP), creating opportunities for rooftop podiums while protecting Allan Border Oval from additional overshadowing.

Design Guidelines:

— Shape building height, massing and upper level setbacks in accordance with the Allan Border Oval solar clipping planes to prevent additional midwinter overshadowing. — Locate the tallest built form on the existing Council Chambers site in response to the Allan Border Oval solar clipping plane requirements. — Provide a chamfered building edge responding to the Solar Clipping Plane to open views and strengthen visual connections to the Village Green. — Step upper levels in accordance with the Solar Clipping Plane to maximise solar access while creating opportunities for rooftop podiums. — *Potential to extend the Library & Community Hub to a fifth floor to accommodate a restaurant, subject to confirming revised solar controls (see Appendix 1).

Design Guidelines:

— Retain and integrate the frontages of significant heritage terraces along Military Road. Locate new built form above and set back from retained heritage frontages to maintain their prominence, scale and character. — Establish a two-storey streetwall to Military Road and Mosman Square, with upper levels setback 3 metres to reinforce the civic scale of the public domain and reduce perceived building bulk. — Provide a sensitive interface to the neighbouring heritage church through a 3 metre ground floor setback and a 6 metre upper level setback along the eastern boundary — Provide internal pedestrian arcades to break up larger building blocks, including an east–west connection through the Council Chambers site and a north–south connection between Military Road, the Library & Community Hub and The Crescent. — Provide an open-to-sky pedestrian link aligned with the principal Library & Community Hub entrance to maintain views and visual connections to Allan Border Oval. — Retain and enhance the heritage significance of the Mosman Art Gallery through sensitive building upgrades and complementary landscape improvements.

### MOSMAN ART GALLERY UPGRADES

### Precedent Imagery - Mosman Art Gallery Upgrades

A concept design is currently being progressed for the Mosman Art Gallery and its surrounding landscape, led by Studio Rewild and Archer Office. The proposal includes enhanced accessibility through new ramps, stairs and elevated walkways, together with new planting, seating, signage, lighting and outdoor gathering spaces that strengthen the Gallery’s landscape setting and support community use. The Civic Place Plan complements this work by strengthening connections between the Art Gallery and the broader Civic Precinct, including future integration with a proposed shared zone along Art Gallery Way to enhance pedestrian connectivity and amenity.

STAGES 1-3

The Mosman Civic Precinct Place Plan establishes a local and community-focused precinct centred on a new Library & Community Hub, an expanded Village Green, upgrades to the Mosman Art Gallery and enhanced public realm. A mix of community facilities, commercial uses and active ground floor frontages supports long-term activation, while new public spaces and pedestrian connections strengthen civic identity, improve accessibility and create a vibrant destination for the Mosman community.

Assumptions:

— GFA calculations are based on the site boundary highlighted below. — Commercial GFA assumes approximately 90% efficiency to account for building cores and circulation. — Development yields are indicative and subject to detailed design, feasibility testing and statutory approvals. — This plan assumes the acquisition or co-development of an adjoining site because this maximises the public benefit outcome and preserves adjacent heritage values. Should this not proceed, an amended Stage 2 is feasible and would be progressed working solely in Council’s own land holdings.

### Indicative Ground Floor Activation Plan

#### KEY

Community uses Commercial uses Heritage building Outdoor performance space Primary pedestrian entry

Upper Ground (Village Green) & Lower Ground (The Crescent) transition

The indicative Ground Floor Activation Plan demonstrates how the Place Plan framework can be realised through a coordinated arrangement of community, commercial and civic uses integrated with a high-quality public realm. The reference scheme illustrates one potential ground floor outcome that prioritises pedestrian movement, active frontages and a connected civic landscape while remaining flexible to respond to future design development.

The Village Green forms the civic heart of the precinct, anchored by a flexible central lawn for markets, informal recreation and everyday use. A dedicated children’s playground provides opportunities for play and family activity, while an outdoor performance space with terraced seating supports community events and performances. Landscaped gathering spaces, informal seating and outdoor dining spill-out areas activate the edges of the Village Green, strengthening the relationship between the public domain, surrounding ground floor uses and the Mosman Art Gallery.

Ground level movement is anchored by the primary north– south civic spine through Mosman Square, supported by a secondary north–south connection extending from Military Road through the Library & Community Hub to the Village Green. The principal Library & Community Hub entry is positioned at the intersection of these civic spaces, reinforcing it as the precinct’s primary arrival point. An east–west arcade linking Mosman Square with the Library & Community Hub further improves pedestrian permeability, creating convenient through-site connections while strengthening access to key destinations.

Together, the coordinated arrangement of uses, landscape and movement establishes an active, welcoming and connected civic destination that strengthens Mosman’s civic heart.

Ground Floor

— Main Library entry level with level connection to the Village Green. — Opportunity to include a cafe beside Village Green. — Grand steps at South-West corner connect levels while providing an open forum for talks and events.

### Illustrative Place Plan

The Illustrative Place Plan demonstrates how the Place Plan framework can be realised through an integrated landscape, public domain and built form response, centred around a new Library & Community Hub, the Village Green and the Mosman Art Gallery. The plan establishes a connected civic destination where landscape defines the public realm, strengthens civic identity and reinforces Mosman’s village character.

A clear hierarchy of civic spaces and pedestrian connections strengthens links between Military Road, Mosman Square, the Village Green and Allan Border Oval. New development is carefully integrated within the landscape framework, responding to the site’s topography, heritage setting and surrounding neighbourhood while providing a flexible framework for future renewal and staged delivery.

### Environmental Assessment

### Solar Access to Open Space

Village Green

Village Green

Village Green

Winter Solstice - 21 June (9am-3pm)

During the winter solstice, only a small portion of the Village Green receives two hours or more of direct sunlight, with most of the space receiving between zero and two hours or no direct sunlight. The most shaded area is located within the proposed children’s playground, where increased shade provides a more comfortable environment for play while preserving greater solar access to the primary lawn and gathering spaces.

Equinox - 21 March (9am-3pm)

During the equinox, solar access improves significantly, with the majority of the Village Green receiving two hours or more of direct sunlight. Areas receiving less than two hours or no direct sunlight are generally confined to the children’s playground, where additional shade supports user comfort without affecting the primary open lawn.

Summer Solstice - 21 December (9am-3pm)

During the summer solstice, almost the entire Village Green receives two hours or more of direct sunlight. The limited shaded area is concentrated within the proposed children’s playground, providing a comfortable setting for play during the warmer months while maintaining excellent solar access across the remainder of the open space.

### Review of Proposed Solar Controls

Proposed Solar Controls No additional overshadowing across Allan Border Oval and its curtilage.

Protects the entire open space from additional overshadowing.

Alternative Solar Controls No additional overshadowing to the sports oval only.

Protects the sports oval while allowing a defined level of additional overshadowing to the surrounding open space (subject to further testing).

KEY

Site boundary

Council-owned land

No additional overshadowing

The proposed solar controls protect the entirety of Allan Border Oval from any additional overshadowing. While this approach preserves solar access across the open space, it also significantly constrains built form opportunities within the Civic Precinct.

This assessment explores alternative solar controls focused on protecting the sports oval only, enabling a greater level of additional development capacity while allowing a defined level of additional overshadowing to the surrounding open space. The pages that follow assess development options under each solar control scenario.

Tests the maximum development potential under the alternative solar controls.

Tests a 4,000m² Library & Community Hub within the proposed solar controls.

Refines the maximum development scenario to achieve a more balanced urban design outcome.

### Solar Controls Review

### Option A (Proposed Solar Controls) - Precinct Plan Scheme

More detailed solar testing has been undertaken to ensure no additional overshadowing of Allan Border Oval in accordance with the proposed solar controls. The updated built form in Option A demonstrates the maximum development potential achievable within these controls while delivering a refined urban design response through improved height transitions, stronger relationships to surrounding public spaces and enhanced view corridors.

Although this represents a balanced outcome under the proposed controls, revising the solar controls would provide an opportunity to increase development capacity, deliver a larger and more flexible Library & Community Hub, and better align with the built form envisaged by the Mosman Masterplan.

10

Library & Community Hub (~3,800m²) retained at 4 storeys to respond to solar controls and create a sensitive 3-storey interface with the Village Green.

2-storey streetwall retained along Mosman Square

2-storey podium creates a consistent built form interface with the Library & Community Hub along the Village Green

Military Road frontage refined to step from 10 → 7 → 5 storeys, improving the transition to the church interface and better aligning with the 5-storey development opposite

Building curvature enables 7 storeys beneath the solar clipping planes while opening views towards the Village Green, Allan Border Oval and the Pavilion COMMERCIAL GFA

### Solar Controls Review

### Option B (Proposed Solar Controls) - 4,000m² Library & Community Hub

This option retains the same built form strategy as Option A while achieving a 4,000m² Library & Community Hub within the proposed solar controls.

An upper-level extension provides the additional floor area required to achieve the 4,000m² library while maintaining the through-site link and preserving key views to Allan Border Oval. The upper-level extension is indicative only and may be achieved through a cantilever, columns, or another structural solution as part of the detailed design.

### Solar Controls Review

Testing under the alternative solar controls

Option D refines the maximum development envelope to deliver a balanced urban design outcome. Reduced building heights improve the overall composition of the precinct while retaining a significant uplift in development capacity (+4,700m² GFA).

The preferred scheme also provides a larger and more flexible Library & Community Hub and building heights that better respond to the future built form envisaged by the Mosman Masterplan.

9

Library & Community Hub increased to approximately 4,700m² while remaining at 4 storeys to maintain a strong civic interface and minimise overshadowing of Allan Border Oval.

Military Road frontage reduced from 12/13 → 9 → 6 storeys to improve built form transition, reduce visual prominence and strengthen the relationship with surrounding development.

Council Chambers redevelopment retained at 12 storeys to reinforce the Military Road corner and respond to the future built form envisaged by the Mosman Masterplan.
